        Idea:
# Pre-flight Key Probe and Key-Aware Worker Distribution

## Context

We recently spent hours diagnosing a 10-task SWE-bench experiment that "collapsed due to quota exhaustion" — only to discover that 3 of our 5 Z.AI API keys were actually healthy. The dead keys were pinned to specific workers by the launch-time distribution, so those workers spun on 429s and died, while the alive keys sat underused. A 5-second `chat/completions` probe with `max_tokens: 1` would have told us the truth immediately.

This plan adds a pre-flight key probe to the eval launch sequence and makes the worker distribution **key-aware**: it skips dead keys, reassigns workers to healthy ones from the pool, and refuses to launch any workers if no healthy keys remain.

## Project directory

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent` — this is a hermes-agent change, not a megaplan change. The megaplan editable install is unaffected.

## Goal

A lightweight, proactive key-health system that runs at launch and (optionally) on-demand for diagnostics.

**Behavior:**

1. Before workers spawn, probe every key in `auto_improve/api_keys.json` via a 1-token `chat/completions` call.
2. Classify each key as **alive**, **exhausted** (429), **invalid** (401/403), or **unreachable** (timeout/network). Capture the reset time from exhausted-key error responses when available.
3. Distribute only **alive** keys to workers. If the number of alive keys is less than the requested worker count, scale the worker count down to `len(alive_keys)` and warn loudly.
4. If **zero keys are alive**, refuse to launch at all. Exit with a clear error and a table of per-key status.
5. Expose the same probe as a standalone CLI: `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` — prints the status table, exits 0 if any key is alive, exits 1 otherwise. This is the diagnostic operators can run from the cron runbook when the cron reports stall or widespread 429s.

## What exists today

- `auto_improve/api_keys.json` — the Z.AI key pool. JSON array of `{"key": "<value>", ...}` entries. Source of truth.
- `megaplan/key_pool.py` — megaplan's in-subprocess key pool. Reads the same JSON file, handles LRU selection, cooldown on 429s. **Do not modify** — this runs inside megaplan subprocesses and is already working. The probe is a separate layer.
- `evals/parallel.py` — the parallel worker launcher. Already has a "Megaplan pre-flight check" step around the worker-spawn logic and already does a "Distributing N API keys across M workers" step. This is where the new probe integrates.
- `~/.hermes/.env` — contains Z.AI base URL and possibly additional keys via `ZHIPU_API_KEY` (and `_1`, `_2`, etc.). The probe must check both sources, matching the same resolution order as `megaplan/key_pool.py::_collect_key_values` and `_load_api_keys_json`.
- The 1-char probe proof-of-concept has already been validated interactively:
  ```python
  # POST https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions
  # body: {"model": "glm-5.1", "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"hi"}], "max_tokens": 1}
  # 200 -> alive, 429 -> exhausted, 401 -> invalid, timeout -> unreachable
  ```

## What needs to exist

### 1. New module: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`

A standalone module with:

- `@dataclass KeyStatus`: fields `key: str`, `provider: str`, `status: Literal["alive", "exhausted", "invalid", "unreachable"]`, `detail: str` (raw error message or summary), `reset_at: str | None` (parsed from 429 response when present), `masked_key: str` (first 8 + last 4 characters, for logging without leaking secrets).

- `load_candidate_keys(provider: str = "zhipu") -> list[str]`: deduped union of keys from `auto_improve/api_keys.json` and `~/.hermes/.env` (matching the env-var resolution that `megaplan/key_pool.py` uses — `ZHIPU_API_KEY`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_1`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_2`, etc.). No network calls.

- `probe_key(key: str, *, provider: str = "zhipu", timeout: float = 15.0) -> KeyStatus`: single-key probe. For `zhipu`, POST to `https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions` with model `glm-5.1`, a one-character user message, and `max_tokens: 1`. Map HTTP codes and exceptions to the `KeyStatus` taxonomy. Parse reset time from 429 body via regex `r"reset at ([0-9T :\-\.]+)"`.

- `probe_all_keys(provider: str = "zhipu", *, concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 15.0) -> list[KeyStatus]`: runs `probe_key` for every candidate via a `ThreadPoolExecutor` so N keys take ~max(per-call-time) not N×per-call. Returns results in the same order as the input list.

- `format_status_table(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> str`: pretty-printed table for the CLI: `key`, `worker assignment` (from the distribution arg if provided), `status`, `detail (truncated)`, `reset_at`. Uses unicode check/cross marks.

- `alive_keys(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]`: the raw key strings from entries whose status is `alive`.

- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: CLI entry point. Prints the status table, exits 0 if at least one key is alive, 1 if none are. Accept flags: `--provider zhipu|minimax` (default `zhipu`), `--concurrency N`, `--timeout SECONDS`, `--json` (print JSON instead of table). Module must be runnable as `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`.

Do NOT probe MiniMax keys in this PR unless it's trivial to add. Z.AI is the critical path.

### 2. Integrate the probe into `evals/parallel.py`

Locate the current pre-flight and key-distribution section — around where the launch log prints:
```
[parallel] Megaplan pre-flight check passed
[parallel] Distributing N API keys across M workers
  worker-0: key=xxxxxx... base_url=...
```

Add a new step immediately after "Megaplan pre-flight check passed" and BEFORE key distribution:

1. Call `probe_all_keys("zhipu")`.
2. Print a status line per key, using the masked form: `  [alive] 6a254d67... (worker slot: w0)` / `  [exhausted] 637742fa... reset 2026-04-08 11:55:35`.
3. Filter candidate keys for distribution to `alive` keys only.
4. If `len(alive) < requested_workers`: log a warning, scale `requested_workers` to `len(alive)`, and continue. The warning message must include the count of dead keys and their failure categories.
5. If `len(alive) == 0`: raise a clear, user-facing exception with the full status table and exit the parallel runner. No workers launch.
6. The existing "Distributing N API keys across M workers" line uses the filtered alive list from step 3. Worker N gets `alive_keys[N % len(alive_keys)]`.

No changes to how workers receive the key once assigned — they still get it via the existing config/env path. The filtering happens before distribution.

### 3. Runbook update

Edit `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`:

- In the "Zero-progress stall" section, add as the first diagnostic step: "Run `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`. If the table shows <50% keys alive, do not restart workers — quota is the bottleneck and restarts will just burn cycles on dead keys. Wait for the latest reset time."
- Add a new "Known issues" row: `Dead API key in pool | Workers spin on 429s, launch silently scales down | Run probe_keys; check api_keys.json against its backup`.

### 4. Tests

- `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py` — unit tests for the module. Must NOT hit the real Z.AI endpoint. Use `unittest.mock` to stub `urllib.request.urlopen` and return fake `200`, `429` (with reset-time body), `401`, timeout exceptions. Tests:
  - `probe_key` classifies 200 as `alive`
  - `probe_key` classifies 429 as `exhausted` with `reset_at` parsed from body
  - `probe_key` classifies 401 as `invalid`
  - `probe_key` classifies `socket.timeout` as `unreachable`
  - `load_candidate_keys` dedupes env + json
  - `alive_keys` filter returns only alive entries
  - `main` exits 0 when any alive, 1 when none
  - `format_status_table` masks secrets (raw key must not appear in output)
  - Concurrency test: `probe_all_keys` with 5 keys finishes in under 2× single-probe wall time

- `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` — integration test for the parallel.py changes. Mock `probe_all_keys` to return synthetic results (e.g. 3 alive + 2 dead from a pool of 5). Assert that:
  - Launch with 5-alive pool uses the existing 4-worker path
  - Launch with 3-alive pool scales workers from 4 to 3 and prints a warning
  - Launch with 0-alive pool raises and does NOT call the worker-spawn function
  - Assert the masked key format is in the log, NOT the raw key

Run the full `pytest` suite as a regression guard at the end.

## Out of scope

- MiniMax key probing (can come in a later PR — structurally identical but lower priority).
- Changes to `megaplan/key_pool.py` internal rotation logic.
- Automatic re-probe during a running eval (probe is launch-time only; cron runbook covers on-demand diagnostic).
- Any changes to iteration-021 or iteration-022-robust data.
- Changes to how individual megaplan subprocesses acquire keys from the pool — they continue to use the existing LRU/cooldown logic inside megaplan.

## Success criteria

- `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` runs in under 10 seconds, prints a table, exits 0/1 correctly.
- `evals/parallel.py` refuses to launch when all keys are dead and logs a clear reason.
- `evals/parallel.py` scales workers down silently-but-loudly when some keys are dead.
- Raw API keys never appear in any log output — only masked form (`6a254d67...1UDf`).
- No change to behavior when all keys are alive (existing launch path unchanged for the happy case).
- Full test suite passes including the new ones.
- The standalone probe matches reality: running it right now against our current pool must correctly identify `637742fa` and `d967d4c1` as exhausted (these are the two dead keys we've been debugging).

  "meta_commentary": "Critical gotcha: FLAG-001 \u2014 the probe must run in the CALLERS (run_parallel_workers and join_parallel_run) before manifest.reserve_*, not inside _launch_worker_processes. If you are tempted to put it in the launcher because 'that's where key distribution happens', stop: the manifest, pidfile, and join summaries will describe workers that were never spawned. The regression test in T4 (e/f) exists specifically to catch this \u2014 do not delete or weaken it.\\n\\nFLAG-002 is an accepted tradeoff: the probe hardcodes https://api.z.ai/... and discards base_url when constructing raw_keys. However, when FILTERING api_keys down to alive_entries, you must keep the original dict entries (with their base_url) \u2014 iterate over api_keys and keep those whose 'key' field is in the alive set. Do NOT rebuild entries from raw keys. This preserves base_url pairing for the happy path while accepting that non-default-endpoint keys may be misclassified (documented in the brief).\\n\\nPatching urlopen: patch at the auto_improve.probe_keys namespace (`mock.patch('auto_improve.probe_keys.urllib.request.urlopen')` or import urlopen into the module and patch there). Patching globally in urllib.request can leak across tests.\\n\\nHTTPError construction: httperror requires fp argument. Use `urllib.error.HTTPError(url, 429, 'Too Many Requests', {}, io.BytesIO(b'quota exhausted, reset at 2026-04-08 11:55:35'))` or similar.\\n\\nMasking edge cases: for short keys (< 12 chars), key[:8]+'...'+key[-4:] may overlap. Handle gracefully (e.g., if len(key) < 12 just return key[:4]+'...'). Test keys in fixtures should be long enough to avoid this.\\n\\nEnv parsing: simple line scanner \u2014 strip comments, handle `KEY=value` and `KEY='value'` and `KEY=\\\"value\\\"`, skip blanks. Do not pull in python-dotenv.\\n\\nHappy-path byte-identity is a MUST: when all keys alive, the existing distribution at ~evals/parallel.py:558-579 must produce identical worker\u2192key assignments as before. Keep filtering order stable.\\n\\nCLI budget: default timeout=5.0s, concurrency=8, so N\u22648 keys finish in one batch \u22645s, well under the 10s target.\\n\\nDo not touch megaplan/key_pool.py, iteration-021/022 data, or _preflight_check_megaplan() placement. Keep the diff tight.",

name, value = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n+        name = name.strip()\n+        value = value.strip()\n+        if not name:\n+            continue\n+        if value and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] not in seen:\n+            seen.add(key)\n+            ordered.append(key)\n+\n+    env_map = _load_env_map(Path.home() / \".hermes\" / \".env\")\n+    env_names = [\"ZHIPU_API_KEY\", seen.add(key)\n+            ordered.append(key)\n+\n+    return ordered\n+\n+\n+def probe_key(key: str, *, provider: str = \"zhipu\", timeout: float = 5.0) -> KeyStatus:\n+ provider=provider,\n+                status=\"alive\",\n+                detail=detail,\n+                reset_at=None,\n+                masked_key=_mask_key(key),\n+            )\n+    except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:\n+        body = exc.read().decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n+        reset_at_match = RESET_AT_PATTERN.search(body)\n+        reset_at = reset_at_match.group(1) elif exc.code in {401, 403}:\n+            status = \"invalid\"\n+        else:\n+            status = \"unreachable\"\n+        return KeyStatus(\n+            key=key,\n+            provider=provider,\n+            status=status,\n+            detail=detail,\n+            reset_at=reset_at,\n+            masked_key=_mask_key(key),\n+        )\n+    except socket.timeout:\n+        detail = _short_detail(\"socket timeout\", key=key)\n+    except urllib.error.URLError as exc:\n+        detail = _short_detail(f\"url error: {exc.reason}\", key=key)\n+    except Exception as exc: concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 5.0\n+) -> list[KeyStatus]:\n+    _require_provider(provider)\n+    if not keys:\n+        return []\n+\n+    results: list[KeyStatus | None] = [None] * len(keys)\n+    max_workers = max(1, min(concurrency, len(keys)))\n+    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:\n+        future_to_index = {\n+            executor.submit(probe_key, key, provider=provider, timeout=timeout): index\n+            for index, key in enumerate(keys)\n+        }\n+        for future in as_completed(future_to_index):\n+            results[future_to_index[future]] = future.result()\n+    return [result for result in results if result is not None]\n+\n+\n+def probe_all_keys(provider: str = \"zhipu\", *, concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 5.0) -> list[KeyStatus]:\n+    return probe_keys(\n+        load_candidate_keys(provider=provider),\n+        provider=provider,\n+        concurrency=concurrency,\n+        timeout=timeout,\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def alive_keys(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]:\n+    return [result.key for result in results if result.status == \"alive\"]\n+\n+\n+def format_status_table(results: list[KeyStatus], *, assignments: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> str:\n+    assignments = assignments or {}\n+    headers = (\"key\", \"worker\", \"status\", \"detail\", \"reset_at\")\n+    rows: list[tuple[str, str, str, str, str]] = []\n+    for result in results:\n+        status_text = (\"✓\" if result.status == \"alive\" else \"✗\") + f\" {result.status}\"\n+        detail = result.detail[:TABLE_DETAIL_LIMIT]\n+        rows.append(\n+            (\n+                result.masked_key,\n+                assignments.get(result.key, \"-\"),\n+                status_text,\n+                detail,\n+                result.reset_at or \"-\",\n+            )\n+        )\n+\n+    widths = [len(header) for header in headers]\n+    for row in rows:\n+        for index, cell in enumerate(row):\n+            widths[index] = max(widths[index], len(cell))\n+\n+    lines = [\n+        \"  \".join(header.ljust(widths[index]) for index, header in enumerate(headers)),\n+        \"  \".join(\"-\" * width for width in widths),\n+    ]\n+    lines.extend(\"  \".join(cell.ljust(widths[index]) for index, cell in enumerate(row)) for row in rows)\n+    table = \"\\n\".join(lines)\n+    for result in results:\n+        assert result.key not in table\n+    return table\n+\n+\n+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Probe configured API keys.\")\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--provider\", default=\"zhipu\")\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--concurrency\", type=int, default=8)\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--timeout\", type=float, default=5.0)\n+    parser.add_argument(\"--json\", action=\"store_true\", dest=\"json_output\")\n+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n+\n+    results = probe_all_keys(provider=args.provider, concurrency=args.concurrency, timeout=args.timeout)\n+    if args.json_output:\n+        print(json.dumps([asdict(result) for result in results], indent=2))\n+    else:\n+        print(format_status_table(results))\n+    return 0 if alive_keys(results) else 1\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    sys.exit(main())\n*** End Patch\n"

        Idea:
# Pre-flight Key Probe and Key-Aware Worker Distribution

## Context

We recently spent hours diagnosing a 10-task SWE-bench experiment that "collapsed due to quota exhaustion" — only to discover that 3 of our 5 Z.AI API keys were actually healthy. The dead keys were pinned to specific workers by the launch-time distribution, so those workers spun on 429s and died, while the alive keys sat underused. A 5-second `chat/completions` probe with `max_tokens: 1` would have told us the truth immediately.

This plan adds a pre-flight key probe to the eval launch sequence and makes the worker distribution **key-aware**: it skips dead keys, reassigns workers to healthy ones from the pool, and refuses to launch any workers if no healthy keys remain.

## Project directory

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent` — this is a hermes-agent change, not a megaplan change. The megaplan editable install is unaffected.

## Goal

A lightweight, proactive key-health system that runs at launch and (optionally) on-demand for diagnostics.

**Behavior:**

1. Before workers spawn, probe every key in `auto_improve/api_keys.json` via a 1-token `chat/completions` call.
2. Classify each key as **alive**, **exhausted** (429), **invalid** (401/403), or **unreachable** (timeout/network). Capture the reset time from exhausted-key error responses when available.
3. Distribute only **alive** keys to workers. If the number of alive keys is less than the requested worker count, scale the worker count down to `len(alive_keys)` and warn loudly.
4. If **zero keys are alive**, refuse to launch at all. Exit with a clear error and a table of per-key status.
5. Expose the same probe as a standalone CLI: `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` — prints the status table, exits 0 if any key is alive, exits 1 otherwise. This is the diagnostic operators can run from the cron runbook when the cron reports stall or widespread 429s.

## What exists today

- `auto_improve/api_keys.json` — the Z.AI key pool. JSON array of `{"key": "<value>", ...}` entries. Source of truth.
- `megaplan/key_pool.py` — megaplan's in-subprocess key pool. Reads the same JSON file, handles LRU selection, cooldown on 429s. **Do not modify** — this runs inside megaplan subprocesses and is already working. The probe is a separate layer.
- `evals/parallel.py` — the parallel worker launcher. Already has a "Megaplan pre-flight check" step around the worker-spawn logic and already does a "Distributing N API keys across M workers" step. This is where the new probe integrates.
- `~/.hermes/.env` — contains Z.AI base URL and possibly additional keys via `ZHIPU_API_KEY` (and `_1`, `_2`, etc.). The probe must check both sources, matching the same resolution order as `megaplan/key_pool.py::_collect_key_values` and `_load_api_keys_json`.
- The 1-char probe proof-of-concept has already been validated interactively:
  ```python
  # POST https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions
  # body: {"model": "glm-5.1", "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"hi"}], "max_tokens": 1}
  # 200 -> alive, 429 -> exhausted, 401 -> invalid, timeout -> unreachable
  ```

## What needs to exist

### 1. New module: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`

A standalone module with:

- `@dataclass KeyStatus`: fields `key: str`, `provider: str`, `status: Literal["alive", "exhausted", "invalid", "unreachable"]`, `detail: str` (raw error message or summary), `reset_at: str | None` (parsed from 429 response when present), `masked_key: str` (first 8 + last 4 characters, for logging without leaking secrets).

- `load_candidate_keys(provider: str = "zhipu") -> list[str]`: deduped union of keys from `auto_improve/api_keys.json` and `~/.hermes/.env` (matching the env-var resolution that `megaplan/key_pool.py` uses — `ZHIPU_API_KEY`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_1`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_2`, etc.). No network calls.

- `probe_key(key: str, *, provider: str = "zhipu", timeout: float = 15.0) -> KeyStatus`: single-key probe. For `zhipu`, POST to `https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions` with model `glm-5.1`, a one-character user message, and `max_tokens: 1`. Map HTTP codes and exceptions to the `KeyStatus` taxonomy. Parse reset time from 429 body via regex `r"reset at ([0-9T :\-\.]+)"`.

- `probe_all_keys(provider: str = "zhipu", *, concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 15.0) -> list[KeyStatus]`: runs `probe_key` for every candidate via a `ThreadPoolExecutor` so N keys take ~max(per-call-time) not N×per-call. Returns results in the same order as the input list.

- `format_status_table(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> str`: pretty-printed table for the CLI: `key`, `worker assignment` (from the distribution arg if provided), `status`, `detail (truncated)`, `reset_at`. Uses unicode check/cross marks.

- `alive_keys(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]`: the raw key strings from entries whose status is `alive`.

- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: CLI entry point. Prints the status table, exits 0 if at least one key is alive, 1 if none are. Accept flags: `--provider zhipu|minimax` (default `zhipu`), `--concurrency N`, `--timeout SECONDS`, `--json` (print JSON instead of table). Module must be runnable as `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`.

Do NOT probe MiniMax keys in this PR unless it's trivial to add. Z.AI is the critical path.

### 2. Integrate the probe into `evals/parallel.py`

Locate the current pre-flight and key-distribution section — around where the launch log prints:
```
[parallel] Megaplan pre-flight check passed
[parallel] Distributing N API keys across M workers
  worker-0: key=xxxxxx... base_url=...
```

Add a new step immediately after "Megaplan pre-flight check passed" and BEFORE key distribution:

1. Call `probe_all_keys("zhipu")`.
2. Print a status line per key, using the masked form: `  [alive] 6a254d67... (worker slot: w0)` / `  [exhausted] 637742fa... reset 2026-04-08 11:55:35`.
3. Filter candidate keys for distribution to `alive` keys only.
4. If `len(alive) < requested_workers`: log a warning, scale `requested_workers` to `len(alive)`, and continue. The warning message must include the count of dead keys and their failure categories.
5. If `len(alive) == 0`: raise a clear, user-facing exception with the full status table and exit the parallel runner. No workers launch.
6. The existing "Distributing N API keys across M workers" line uses the filtered alive list from step 3. Worker N gets `alive_keys[N % len(alive_keys)]`.

No changes to how workers receive the key once assigned — they still get it via the existing config/env path. The filtering happens before distribution.

### 3. Runbook update

Edit `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`:

- In the "Zero-progress stall" section, add as the first diagnostic step: "Run `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`. If the table shows <50% keys alive, do not restart workers — quota is the bottleneck and restarts will just burn cycles on dead keys. Wait for the latest reset time."
- Add a new "Known issues" row: `Dead API key in pool | Workers spin on 429s, launch silently scales down | Run probe_keys; check api_keys.json against its backup`.

### 4. Tests

- `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py` — unit tests for the module. Must NOT hit the real Z.AI endpoint. Use `unittest.mock` to stub `urllib.request.urlopen` and return fake `200`, `429` (with reset-time body), `401`, timeout exceptions. Tests:
  - `probe_key` classifies 200 as `alive`
  - `probe_key` classifies 429 as `exhausted` with `reset_at` parsed from body
  - `probe_key` classifies 401 as `invalid`
  - `probe_key` classifies `socket.timeout` as `unreachable`
  - `load_candidate_keys` dedupes env + json
  - `alive_keys` filter returns only alive entries
  - `main` exits 0 when any alive, 1 when none
  - `format_status_table` masks secrets (raw key must not appear in output)
  - Concurrency test: `probe_all_keys` with 5 keys finishes in under 2× single-probe wall time

- `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` — integration test for the parallel.py changes. Mock `probe_all_keys` to return synthetic results (e.g. 3 alive + 2 dead from a pool of 5). Assert that:
  - Launch with 5-alive pool uses the existing 4-worker path
  - Launch with 3-alive pool scales workers from 4 to 3 and prints a warning
  - Launch with 0-alive pool raises and does NOT call the worker-spawn function
  - Assert the masked key format is in the log, NOT the raw key

Run the full `pytest` suite as a regression guard at the end.

## Out of scope

- MiniMax key probing (can come in a later PR — structurally identical but lower priority).
- Changes to `megaplan/key_pool.py` internal rotation logic.
- Automatic re-probe during a running eval (probe is launch-time only; cron runbook covers on-demand diagnostic).
- Any changes to iteration-021 or iteration-022-robust data.
- Changes to how individual megaplan subprocesses acquire keys from the pool — they continue to use the existing LRU/cooldown logic inside megaplan.

## Success criteria

- `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` runs in under 10 seconds, prints a table, exits 0/1 correctly.
- `evals/parallel.py` refuses to launch when all keys are dead and logs a clear reason.
- `evals/parallel.py` scales workers down silently-but-loudly when some keys are dead.
- Raw API keys never appear in any log output — only masked form (`6a254d67...1UDf`).
- No change to behavior when all keys are alive (existing launch path unchanged for the happy case).
- Full test suite passes including the new ones.
- The standalone probe matches reality: running it right now against our current pool must correctly identify `637742fa` and `d967d4c1` as exhausted (these are the two dead keys we've been debugging).

## Files likely to change

- NEW: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`
- NEW: `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py`
- NEW: `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` (or extend existing parallel tests if present)
- MODIFIED: `evals/parallel.py` — add probe call, filter distribution, refuse-to-launch path
- MODIFIED: `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md` — diagnostic step in stall section + known issues row

  "meta_commentary": "Critical gotcha: FLAG-001 \u2014 the probe must run in the CALLERS (run_parallel_workers and join_parallel_run) before manifest.reserve_*, not inside _launch_worker_processes. If you are tempted to put it in the launcher because 'that's where key distribution happens', stop: the manifest, pidfile, and join summaries will describe workers that were never spawned. The regression test in T4 (e/f) exists specifically to catch this \u2014 do not delete or weaken it.\\n\\nFLAG-002 is an accepted tradeoff: the probe hardcodes https://api.z.ai/... and discards base_url when constructing raw_keys. However, when FILTERING api_keys down to alive_entries, you must keep the original dict entries (with their base_url) \u2014 iterate over api_keys and keep those whose 'key' field is in the alive set. Do NOT rebuild entries from raw keys. This preserves base_url pairing for the happy path while accepting that non-default-endpoint keys may be misclassified (documented in the brief).\\n\\nPatching urlopen: patch at the auto_improve.probe_keys namespace (`mock.patch('auto_improve.probe_keys.urllib.request.urlopen')` or import urlopen into the module and patch there). Patching globally in urllib.request can leak across tests.\\n\\nHTTPError construction: httperror requires fp argument. Use `urllib.error.HTTPError(url, 429, 'Too Many Requests', {}, io.BytesIO(b'quota exhausted, reset at 2026-04-08 11:55:35'))` or similar.\\n\\nMasking edge cases: for short keys (< 12 chars), key[:8]+'...'+key[-4:] may overlap. Handle gracefully (e.g., if len(key) < 12 just return key[:4]+'...'). Test keys in fixtures should be long enough to avoid this.\\n\\nEnv parsing: simple line scanner \u2014 strip comments, handle `KEY=value` and `KEY='value'` and `KEY=\\\"value\\\"`, skip blanks. Do not pull in python-dotenv.\\n\\nHappy-path byte-identity is a MUST: when all keys alive, the existing distribution at ~evals/parallel.py:558-579 must produce identical worker\u2192key assignments as before. Keep filtering order stable.\\n\\nCLI budget: default timeout=5.0s, concurrency=8, so N\u22648 keys finish in one batch \u22645s, well under the 10s target.\\n\\nDo not touch megaplan/key_pool.py, iteration-021/022 data, or _preflight_check_megaplan() placement. Keep the diff tight.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `extra_run_args` passthrough against `tools/environments/docker.py:106-116,318`. appending extra args after `_security_args` does not explain how the existing `--tmpfs /tmp...`, `--tmpfs /var/tmp...`, and `--pids-limit 256` entries are removed or overridden, so the revised plan still does not technically solve the parity problem it claims to address. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new patch-file design against current control flow. because `evals/run_evals.py:927-944` reads the prompt before any proposed prebuild hook and `read_prompt()` still takes only `(task_name, source_root)`, the plan's idea of conditionally instructing the agent to emit `/hermes_output/model_patch.diff` is not wired to the actual runtime order and remains technically under-specified. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised required-key validation idea against the real validators. in this repo `validate_payload()` only checks top-level key presence in `megaplan/workers.py:221-227`, while the proposed critique template would already contain `checks`, `flags`, `verified_flag_ids`, and `disputed_flag_ids`, and `_schema_template()` already emits all required top-level keys for finalize and review in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. an untouched or half-filled template file would therefore still be accepted as the primary payload, then fail later in `validate_critique_checks()` (`megaplan/checks.py:179-220`), `_validate_finalize_payload()` (`megaplan/handlers.py:277-303`), or review merging (`megaplan/handlers.py:989-1025`) without any planned fallthrough back to valid text json. the earlier incomplete-file concern remains open. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked template fidelity after the revision. the plan now fixes critique by switching to `build_empty_template(checks)`, but it still proposes `_schema_template()` for finalize and review even though that helper collapses nested objects to `{}` and nested arrays to `[]` in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. for the two most complex remaining phases, that still gives the model a placeholder file with no task/verdict structure to edit, so the prior shallow-template concern is only partially resolved. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `megaplan/flags.py:57-65` against the new merge rule that unions `verified_flag_ids` and `disputed_flag_ids` across sub-agents. the plan still does not define a conflict policy when different check agents return the same flag id in both lists; today `update_flags_after_critique()` marks verified first and disputed second without clearing the `verified` fields, so one merged contradiction can leave a flag in inconsistent state (`verified: true` but `status: "disputed"`). (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current `score_iteration()` implementation and found the revised `skip-scoring` plan is still technically incomplete. in this repo `score_iteration()` first looks for `_watch_scores.json` and otherwise falls back to `consolidate_with_scoped_logs()`, which requires `_scoring_logs`; a `scoring_mode="none"` run produces neither by itself, so the plan's instruction to still call `score_iteration()` by default after such a run would not reliably work. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 1: the proposed `run_parallel_checks` signature includes `prompt_builder_fn` and `template_writer_fn` but omits the `run_check_fn: callable` callback that the plan text describes as the mechanism for step-specific behavior (agent creation, 429 fallback, parse logic). the critique-specific `_run_check` at parallel_critique.py:40-173 contains logic that cannot be parameterized through prompt/template builders alone — model resolution, minimax/openrouter 429 fallback chain, `parse_agent_output`, and `clean_parsed_payload`. without a `run_check_fn` callback in the signature, the generic primitive cannot correctly handle review's different parse/cleanup needs (review uses different schema and different payload structure than critique). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 4: `_merge_review_verdicts` (handlers.py:1017-1062) only merges `task_verdicts` and `sense_check_verdicts` into `finalize_data` by matching `task_id`/`sense_check_id`. it does not read or merge `review_checks` or `mechanical_prechecks` — these arbitrary payload fields would be stored in `worker.payload` and written to `review.json` but would not influence the verdict-count computation that drives `_resolve_review_outcome`. the plan's phase 4 step 6 says parallel findings are merged before `_merge_review_verdicts`, but the function has no provision for these fields. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 4: mechanical pre-check failures are added only to `worker.payload['issues']` (via `extend`), not to `rework_items`. `_resolve_review_outcome` (handlers.py:1065-1102) determines `needs_rework` based only on `review_verdict`, `verdict_count`, `total_tasks`, `check_count`, `total_checks`, and `missing_evidence` — it does not inspect `issues`. this means a failing mechanical pre-check (e.g., `source_touch` finding no source files modified) would appear in `review.json` but would not automatically trigger the `needs_rework → execute` rework flow unless the llm review independently decides to set `review_verdict = 'needs_rework'`. the plan provides no guaranteed path for mechanical failures to trigger rework. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 3: `diff_size_sanity` is described as comparing 'diff line count to the issue description length as a proxy', but issue text length in characters has no validated correlation with expected diff size in lines. the plan provides no concrete threshold formula (e.g., flag if `added_lines > 10 * (issue_chars / 100)` or a fixed threshold). without a specific heuristic, this check would produce unreliable pass/fail results. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 2: `dead_guard` is described as a 'best-effort heuristic (not a full ast analysis)' using grep to find new `if`/`try` blocks and checking whether the enclosing function appears in at least one call site. this approach cannot determine reachability — a function appearing in a call site does not mean the specific new guard branch is reachable from that call. the plan's own framing ('best-effort') acknowledges this is unreliable, but the pass/fail result would be treated as authoritative in the review phase.         Idea:
# Pre-flight Key Probe and Key-Aware Worker Distribution

## Context

We recently spent hours diagnosing a 10-task SWE-bench experiment that "collapsed due to quota exhaustion" — only to discover that 3 of our 5 Z.AI API keys were actually healthy. The dead keys were pinned to specific workers by the launch-time distribution, so those workers spun on 429s and died, while the alive keys sat underused. A 5-second `chat/completions` probe with `max_tokens: 1` would have told us the truth immediately.

This plan adds a pre-flight key probe to the eval launch sequence and makes the worker distribution **key-aware**: it skips dead keys, reassigns workers to healthy ones from the pool, and refuses to launch any workers if no healthy keys remain.

## Project directory

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent` — this is a hermes-agent change, not a megaplan change. The megaplan editable install is unaffected.

## Goal

A lightweight, proactive key-health system that runs at launch and (optionally) on-demand for diagnostics.

**Behavior:**

1. Before workers spawn, probe every key in `auto_improve/api_keys.json` via a 1-token `chat/completions` call.
2. Classify each key as **alive**, **exhausted** (429), **invalid** (401/403), or **unreachable** (timeout/network). Capture the reset time from exhausted-key error responses when available.
3. Distribute only **alive** keys to workers. If the number of alive keys is less than the requested worker count, scale the worker count down to `len(alive_keys)` and warn loudly.
4. If **zero keys are alive**, refuse to launch at all. Exit with a clear error and a table of per-key status.
5. Expose the same probe as a standalone CLI: `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` — prints the status table, exits 0 if any key is alive, exits 1 otherwise. This is the diagnostic operators can run from the cron runbook when the cron reports stall or widespread 429s.

## What exists today

- `auto_improve/api_keys.json` — the Z.AI key pool. JSON array of `{"key": "<value>", ...}` entries. Source of truth.
- `megaplan/key_pool.py` — megaplan's in-subprocess key pool. Reads the same JSON file, handles LRU selection, cooldown on 429s. **Do not modify** — this runs inside megaplan subprocesses and is already working. The probe is a separate layer.
- `evals/parallel.py` — the parallel worker launcher. Already has a "Megaplan pre-flight check" step around the worker-spawn logic and already does a "Distributing N API keys across M workers" step. This is where the new probe integrates.
- `~/.hermes/.env` — contains Z.AI base URL and possibly additional keys via `ZHIPU_API_KEY` (and `_1`, `_2`, etc.). The probe must check both sources, matching the same resolution order as `megaplan/key_pool.py::_collect_key_values` and `_load_api_keys_json`.
- The 1-char probe proof-of-concept has already been validated interactively:
  ```python
  # POST https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions
  # body: {"model": "glm-5.1", "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"hi"}], "max_tokens": 1}
  # 200 -> alive, 429 -> exhausted, 401 -> invalid, timeout -> unreachable
  ```

## What needs to exist

### 1. New module: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`

A standalone module with:

- `@dataclass KeyStatus`: fields `key: str`, `provider: str`, `status: Literal["alive", "exhausted", "invalid", "unreachable"]`, `detail: str` (raw error message or summary), `reset_at: str | None` (parsed from 429 response when present), `masked_key: str` (first 8 + last 4 characters, for logging without leaking secrets).

- `load_candidate_keys(provider: str = "zhipu") -> list[str]`: deduped union of keys from `auto_improve/api_keys.json` and `~/.hermes/.env` (matching the env-var resolution that `megaplan/key_pool.py` uses — `ZHIPU_API_KEY`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_1`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_2`, etc.). No network calls.

- `probe_key(key: str, *, provider: str = "zhipu", timeout: float = 15.0) -> KeyStatus`: single-key probe. For `zhipu`, POST to `https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions` with model `glm-5.1`, a one-character user message, and `max_tokens: 1`. Map HTTP codes and exceptions to the `KeyStatus` taxonomy. Parse reset time from 429 body via regex `r"reset at ([0-9T :\-\.]+)"`.

- `probe_all_keys(provider: str = "zhipu", *, concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 15.0) -> list[KeyStatus]`: runs `probe_key` for every candidate via a `ThreadPoolExecutor` so N keys take ~max(per-call-time) not N×per-call. Returns results in the same order as the input list.

- `format_status_table(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> str`: pretty-printed table for the CLI: `key`, `worker assignment` (from the distribution arg if provided), `status`, `detail (truncated)`, `reset_at`. Uses unicode check/cross marks.

- `alive_keys(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]`: the raw key strings from entries whose status is `alive`.

- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: CLI entry point. Prints the status table, exits 0 if at least one key is alive, 1 if none are. Accept flags: `--provider zhipu|minimax` (default `zhipu`), `--concurrency N`, `--timeout SECONDS`, `--json` (print JSON instead of table). Module must be runnable as `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`.

Do NOT probe MiniMax keys in this PR unless it's trivial to add. Z.AI is the critical path.

### 2. Integrate the probe into `evals/parallel.py`

Locate the current pre-flight and key-distribution section — around where the launch log prints:
```
[parallel] Megaplan pre-flight check passed
[parallel] Distributing N API keys across M workers
  worker-0: key=xxxxxx... base_url=...
```

Add a new step immediately after "Megaplan pre-flight check passed" and BEFORE key distribution:

1. Call `probe_all_keys("zhipu")`.
2. Print a status line per key, using the masked form: `  [alive] 6a254d67... (worker slot: w0)` / `  [exhausted] 637742fa... reset 2026-04-08 11:55:35`.
3. Filter candidate keys for distribution to `alive` keys only.
4. If `len(alive) < requested_workers`: log a warning, scale `requested_workers` to `len(alive)`, and continue. The warning message must include the count of dead keys and their failure categories.
5. If `len(alive) == 0`: raise a clear, user-facing exception with the full status table and exit the parallel runner. No workers launch.
6. The existing "Distributing N API keys across M workers" line uses the filtered alive list from step 3. Worker N gets `alive_keys[N % len(alive_keys)]`.

No changes to how workers receive the key once assigned — they still get it via the existing config/env path. The filtering happens before distribution.

### 3. Runbook update

Edit `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`:

- In the "Zero-progress stall" section, add as the first diagnostic step: "Run `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`. If the table shows <50% keys alive, do not restart workers — quota is the bottleneck and restarts will just burn cycles on dead keys. Wait for the latest reset time."
- Add a new "Known issues" row: `Dead API key in pool | Workers spin on 429s, launch silently scales down | Run probe_keys; check api_keys.json against its backup`.

### 4. Tests

- `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py` — unit tests for the module. Must NOT hit the real Z.AI endpoint. Use `unittest.mock` to stub `urllib.request.urlopen` and return fake `200`, `429` (with reset-time body), `401`, timeout exceptions. Tests:
  - `probe_key` classifies 200 as `alive`
  - `probe_key` classifies 429 as `exhausted` with `reset_at` parsed from body
  - `probe_key` classifies 401 as `invalid`
  - `probe_key` classifies `socket.timeout` as `unreachable`
  - `load_candidate_keys` dedupes env + json
  - `alive_keys` filter returns only alive entries
  - `main` exits 0 when any alive, 1 when none
  - `format_status_table` masks secrets (raw key must not appear in output)
  - Concurrency test: `probe_all_keys` with 5 keys finishes in under 2× single-probe wall time

- `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` — integration test for the parallel.py changes. Mock `probe_all_keys` to return synthetic results (e.g. 3 alive + 2 dead from a pool of 5). Assert that:
  - Launch with 5-alive pool uses the existing 4-worker path
  - Launch with 3-alive pool scales workers from 4 to 3 and prints a warning
  - Launch with 0-alive pool raises and does NOT call the worker-spawn function
  - Assert the masked key format is in the log, NOT the raw key

Run the full `pytest` suite as a regression guard at the end.

## Out of scope

- MiniMax key probing (can come in a later PR — structurally identical but lower priority).
- Changes to `megaplan/key_pool.py` internal rotation logic.
- Automatic re-probe during a running eval (probe is launch-time only; cron runbook covers on-demand diagnostic).
- Any changes to iteration-021 or iteration-022-robust data.
- Changes to how individual megaplan subprocesses acquire keys from the pool — they continue to use the existing LRU/cooldown logic inside megaplan.

## Success criteria

- `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` runs in under 10 seconds, prints a table, exits 0/1 correctly.
- `evals/parallel.py` refuses to launch when all keys are dead and logs a clear reason.
- `evals/parallel.py` scales workers down silently-but-loudly when some keys are dead.
- Raw API keys never appear in any log output — only masked form (`6a254d67...1UDf`).
- No change to behavior when all keys are alive (existing launch path unchanged for the happy case).
- Full test suite passes including the new ones.
- The standalone probe matches reality: running it right now against our current pool must correctly identify `637742fa` and `d967d4c1` as exhausted (these are the two dead keys we've been debugging).

  "meta_commentary": "Critical gotcha: FLAG-001 \u2014 the probe must run in the CALLERS (run_parallel_workers and join_parallel_run) before manifest.reserve_*, not inside _launch_worker_processes. If you are tempted to put it in the launcher because 'that's where key distribution happens', stop: the manifest, pidfile, and join summaries will describe workers that were never spawned. The regression test in T4 (e/f) exists specifically to catch this \u2014 do not delete or weaken it.\\n\\nFLAG-002 is an accepted tradeoff: the probe hardcodes https://api.z.ai/... and discards base_url when constructing raw_keys. However, when FILTERING api_keys down to alive_entries, you must keep the original dict entries (with their base_url) \u2014 iterate over api_keys and keep those whose 'key' field is in the alive set. Do NOT rebuild entries from raw keys. This preserves base_url pairing for the happy path while accepting that non-default-endpoint keys may be misclassified (documented in the brief).\\n\\nPatching urlopen: patch at the auto_improve.probe_keys namespace (`mock.patch('auto_improve.probe_keys.urllib.request.urlopen')` or import urlopen into the module and patch there). Patching globally in urllib.request can leak across tests.\\n\\nHTTPError construction: httperror requires fp argument. Use `urllib.error.HTTPError(url, 429, 'Too Many Requests', {}, io.BytesIO(b'quota exhausted, reset at 2026-04-08 11:55:35'))` or similar.\\n\\nMasking edge cases: for short keys (< 12 chars), key[:8]+'...'+key[-4:] may overlap. Handle gracefully (e.g., if len(key) < 12 just return key[:4]+'...'). Test keys in fixtures should be long enough to avoid this.\\n\\nEnv parsing: simple line scanner \u2014 strip comments, handle `KEY=value` and `KEY='value'` and `KEY=\\\"value\\\"`, skip blanks. Do not pull in python-dotenv.\\n\\nHappy-path byte-identity is a MUST: when all keys alive, the existing distribution at ~evals/parallel.py:558-579 must produce identical worker\u2192key assignments as before. Keep filtering order stable.\\n\\nCLI budget: default timeout=5.0s, concurrency=8, so N\u22648 keys finish in one batch \u22645s, well under the 10s target.\\n\\nDo not touch megaplan/key_pool.py, iteration-021/022 data, or _preflight_check_megaplan() placement. Keep the diff tight.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `extra_run_args` passthrough against `tools/environments/docker.py:106-116,318`. appending extra args after `_security_args` does not explain how the existing `--tmpfs /tmp...`, `--tmpfs /var/tmp...`, and `--pids-limit 256` entries are removed or overridden, so the revised plan still does not technically solve the parity problem it claims to address. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new patch-file design against current control flow. because `evals/run_evals.py:927-944` reads the prompt before any proposed prebuild hook and `read_prompt()` still takes only `(task_name, source_root)`, the plan's idea of conditionally instructing the agent to emit `/hermes_output/model_patch.diff` is not wired to the actual runtime order and remains technically under-specified. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised required-key validation idea against the real validators. in this repo `validate_payload()` only checks top-level key presence in `megaplan/workers.py:221-227`, while the proposed critique template would already contain `checks`, `flags`, `verified_flag_ids`, and `disputed_flag_ids`, and `_schema_template()` already emits all required top-level keys for finalize and review in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. an untouched or half-filled template file would therefore still be accepted as the primary payload, then fail later in `validate_critique_checks()` (`megaplan/checks.py:179-220`), `_validate_finalize_payload()` (`megaplan/handlers.py:277-303`), or review merging (`megaplan/handlers.py:989-1025`) without any planned fallthrough back to valid text json. the earlier incomplete-file concern remains open. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked template fidelity after the revision. the plan now fixes critique by switching to `build_empty_template(checks)`, but it still proposes `_schema_template()` for finalize and review even though that helper collapses nested objects to `{}` and nested arrays to `[]` in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. for the two most complex remaining phases, that still gives the model a placeholder file with no task/verdict structure to edit, so the prior shallow-template concern is only partially resolved. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `megaplan/flags.py:57-65` against the new merge rule that unions `verified_flag_ids` and `disputed_flag_ids` across sub-agents. the plan still does not define a conflict policy when different check agents return the same flag id in both lists; today `update_flags_after_critique()` marks verified first and disputed second without clearing the `verified` fields, so one merged contradiction can leave a flag in inconsistent state (`verified: true` but `status: "disputed"`). (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the current `score_iteration()` implementation and found the revised `skip-scoring` plan is still technically incomplete. in this repo `score_iteration()` first looks for `_watch_scores.json` and otherwise falls back to `consolidate_with_scoped_logs()`, which requires `_scoring_logs`; a `scoring_mode="none"` run produces neither by itself, so the plan's instruction to still call `score_iteration()` by default after such a run would not reliably work. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Pre-flight Key Probe and Key-Aware Worker Distribution

## Context

We recently spent hours diagnosing a 10-task SWE-bench experiment that "collapsed due to quota exhaustion" — only to discover that 3 of our 5 Z.AI API keys were actually healthy. The dead keys were pinned to specific workers by the launch-time distribution, so those workers spun on 429s and died, while the alive keys sat underused. A 5-second `chat/completions` probe with `max_tokens: 1` would have told us the truth immediately.

This plan adds a pre-flight key probe to the eval launch sequence and makes the worker distribution **key-aware**: it skips dead keys, reassigns workers to healthy ones from the pool, and refuses to launch any workers if no healthy keys remain.

## Project directory

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent` — this is a hermes-agent change, not a megaplan change. The megaplan editable install is unaffected.

## Goal

A lightweight, proactive key-health system that runs at launch and (optionally) on-demand for diagnostics.

**Behavior:**

1. Before workers spawn, probe every key in `auto_improve/api_keys.json` via a 1-token `chat/completions` call.
2. Classify each key as **alive**, **exhausted** (429), **invalid** (401/403), or **unreachable** (timeout/network). Capture the reset time from exhausted-key error responses when available.
3. Distribute only **alive** keys to workers. If the number of alive keys is less than the requested worker count, scale the worker count down to `len(alive_keys)` and warn loudly.
4. If **zero keys are alive**, refuse to launch at all. Exit with a clear error and a table of per-key status.
5. Expose the same probe as a standalone CLI: `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` — prints the status table, exits 0 if any key is alive, exits 1 otherwise. This is the diagnostic operators can run from the cron runbook when the cron reports stall or widespread 429s.

## What exists today

- `auto_improve/api_keys.json` — the Z.AI key pool. JSON array of `{"key": "<value>", ...}` entries. Source of truth.
- `megaplan/key_pool.py` — megaplan's in-subprocess key pool. Reads the same JSON file, handles LRU selection, cooldown on 429s. **Do not modify** — this runs inside megaplan subprocesses and is already working. The probe is a separate layer.
- `evals/parallel.py` — the parallel worker launcher. Already has a "Megaplan pre-flight check" step around the worker-spawn logic and already does a "Distributing N API keys across M workers" step. This is where the new probe integrates.
- `~/.hermes/.env` — contains Z.AI base URL and possibly additional keys via `ZHIPU_API_KEY` (and `_1`, `_2`, etc.). The probe must check both sources, matching the same resolution order as `megaplan/key_pool.py::_collect_key_values` and `_load_api_keys_json`.
- The 1-char probe proof-of-concept has already been validated interactively:
  ```python
  # POST https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions
  # body: {"model": "glm-5.1", "messages": [{"role":"user","content":"hi"}], "max_tokens": 1}
  # 200 -> alive, 429 -> exhausted, 401 -> invalid, timeout -> unreachable
  ```

## What needs to exist

### 1. New module: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`

A standalone module with:

- `@dataclass KeyStatus`: fields `key: str`, `provider: str`, `status: Literal["alive", "exhausted", "invalid", "unreachable"]`, `detail: str` (raw error message or summary), `reset_at: str | None` (parsed from 429 response when present), `masked_key: str` (first 8 + last 4 characters, for logging without leaking secrets).

- `load_candidate_keys(provider: str = "zhipu") -> list[str]`: deduped union of keys from `auto_improve/api_keys.json` and `~/.hermes/.env` (matching the env-var resolution that `megaplan/key_pool.py` uses — `ZHIPU_API_KEY`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_1`, `ZHIPU_API_KEY_2`, etc.). No network calls.

- `probe_key(key: str, *, provider: str = "zhipu", timeout: float = 15.0) -> KeyStatus`: single-key probe. For `zhipu`, POST to `https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4/chat/completions` with model `glm-5.1`, a one-character user message, and `max_tokens: 1`. Map HTTP codes and exceptions to the `KeyStatus` taxonomy. Parse reset time from 429 body via regex `r"reset at ([0-9T :\-\.]+)"`.

- `probe_all_keys(provider: str = "zhipu", *, concurrency: int = 8, timeout: float = 15.0) -> list[KeyStatus]`: runs `probe_key` for every candidate via a `ThreadPoolExecutor` so N keys take ~max(per-call-time) not N×per-call. Returns results in the same order as the input list.

- `format_status_table(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> str`: pretty-printed table for the CLI: `key`, `worker assignment` (from the distribution arg if provided), `status`, `detail (truncated)`, `reset_at`. Uses unicode check/cross marks.

- `alive_keys(results: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]`: the raw key strings from entries whose status is `alive`.

- `main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: CLI entry point. Prints the status table, exits 0 if at least one key is alive, 1 if none are. Accept flags: `--provider zhipu|minimax` (default `zhipu`), `--concurrency N`, `--timeout SECONDS`, `--json` (print JSON instead of table). Module must be runnable as `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`.

Do NOT probe MiniMax keys in this PR unless it's trivial to add. Z.AI is the critical path.

### 2. Integrate the probe into `evals/parallel.py`

Locate the current pre-flight and key-distribution section — around where the launch log prints:
```
[parallel] Megaplan pre-flight check passed
[parallel] Distributing N API keys across M workers
  worker-0: key=xxxxxx... base_url=...
```

Add a new step immediately after "Megaplan pre-flight check passed" and BEFORE key distribution:

1. Call `probe_all_keys("zhipu")`.
2. Print a status line per key, using the masked form: `  [alive] 6a254d67... (worker slot: w0)` / `  [exhausted] 637742fa... reset 2026-04-08 11:55:35`.
3. Filter candidate keys for distribution to `alive` keys only.
4. If `len(alive) < requested_workers`: log a warning, scale `requested_workers` to `len(alive)`, and continue. The warning message must include the count of dead keys and their failure categories.
5. If `len(alive) == 0`: raise a clear, user-facing exception with the full status table and exit the parallel runner. No workers launch.
6. The existing "Distributing N API keys across M workers" line uses the filtered alive list from step 3. Worker N gets `alive_keys[N % len(alive_keys)]`.

No changes to how workers receive the key once assigned — they still get it via the existing config/env path. The filtering happens before distribution.

### 3. Runbook update

Edit `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`:

- In the "Zero-progress stall" section, add as the first diagnostic step: "Run `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`. If the table shows <50% keys alive, do not restart workers — quota is the bottleneck and restarts will just burn cycles on dead keys. Wait for the latest reset time."
- Add a new "Known issues" row: `Dead API key in pool | Workers spin on 429s, launch silently scales down | Run probe_keys; check api_keys.json against its backup`.

### 4. Tests

- `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py` — unit tests for the module. Must NOT hit the real Z.AI endpoint. Use `unittest.mock` to stub `urllib.request.urlopen` and return fake `200`, `429` (with reset-time body), `401`, timeout exceptions. Tests:
  - `probe_key` classifies 200 as `alive`
  - `probe_key` classifies 429 as `exhausted` with `reset_at` parsed from body
  - `probe_key` classifies 401 as `invalid`
  - `probe_key` classifies `socket.timeout` as `unreachable`
  - `load_candidate_keys` dedupes env + json
  - `alive_keys` filter returns only alive entries
  - `main` exits 0 when any alive, 1 when none
  - `format_status_table` masks secrets (raw key must not appear in output)
  - Concurrency test: `probe_all_keys` with 5 keys finishes in under 2× single-probe wall time

- `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` — integration test for the parallel.py changes. Mock `probe_all_keys` to return synthetic results (e.g. 3 alive + 2 dead from a pool of 5). Assert that:
  - Launch with 5-alive pool uses the existing 4-worker path
  - Launch with 3-alive pool scales workers from 4 to 3 and prints a warning
  - Launch with 0-alive pool raises and does NOT call the worker-spawn function
  - Assert the masked key format is in the log, NOT the raw key

Run the full `pytest` suite as a regression guard at the end.

## Out of scope

- MiniMax key probing (can come in a later PR — structurally identical but lower priority).
- Changes to `megaplan/key_pool.py` internal rotation logic.
- Automatic re-probe during a running eval (probe is launch-time only; cron runbook covers on-demand diagnostic).
- Any changes to iteration-021 or iteration-022-robust data.
- Changes to how individual megaplan subprocesses acquire keys from the pool — they continue to use the existing LRU/cooldown logic inside megaplan.

## Success criteria

- `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys` runs in under 10 seconds, prints a table, exits 0/1 correctly.
- `evals/parallel.py` refuses to launch when all keys are dead and logs a clear reason.
- `evals/parallel.py` scales workers down silently-but-loudly when some keys are dead.
- Raw API keys never appear in any log output — only masked form (`6a254d67...1UDf`).
- No change to behavior when all keys are alive (existing launch path unchanged for the happy case).
- Full test suite passes including the new ones.
- The standalone probe matches reality: running it right now against our current pool must correctly identify `637742fa` and `d967d4c1` as exhausted (these are the two dead keys we've been debugging).

## Files likely to change

- NEW: `auto_improve/probe_keys.py`
- NEW: `tests/auto_improve/test_probe_keys.py`
- NEW: `tests/evals/test_parallel_preflight.py` (or extend existing parallel tests if present)
- MODIFIED: `evals/parallel.py` — add probe call, filter distribution, refuse-to-launch path
- MODIFIED: `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md` — diagnostic step in stall section + known issues row

  "meta_commentary": "Critical gotcha: FLAG-001 \u2014 the probe must run in the CALLERS (run_parallel_workers and join_parallel_run) before manifest.reserve_*, not inside _launch_worker_processes. If you are tempted to put it in the launcher because 'that's where key distribution happens', stop: the manifest, pidfile, and join summaries will describe workers that were never spawned. The regression test in T4 (e/f) exists specifically to catch this \u2014 do not delete or weaken it.\\n\\nFLAG-002 is an accepted tradeoff: the probe hardcodes https://api.z.ai/... and discards base_url when constructing raw_keys. However, when FILTERING api_keys down to alive_entries, you must keep the original dict entries (with their base_url) \u2014 iterate over api_keys and keep those whose 'key' field is in the alive set. Do NOT rebuild entries from raw keys. This preserves base_url pairing for the happy path while accepting that non-default-endpoint keys may be misclassified (documented in the brief).\\n\\nPatching urlopen: patch at the auto_improve.probe_keys namespace (`mock.patch('auto_improve.probe_keys.urllib.request.urlopen')` or import urlopen into the module and patch there). Patching globally in urllib.request can leak across tests.\\n\\nHTTPError construction: httperror requires fp argument. Use `urllib.error.HTTPError(url, 429, 'Too Many Requests', {}, io.BytesIO(b'quota exhausted, reset at 2026-04-08 11:55:35'))` or similar.\\n\\nMasking edge cases: for short keys (< 12 chars), key[:8]+'...'+key[-4:] may overlap. Handle gracefully (e.g., if len(key) < 12 just return key[:4]+'...'). Test keys in fixtures should be long enough to avoid this.\\n\\nEnv parsing: simple line scanner \u2014 strip comments, handle `KEY=value` and `KEY='value'` and `KEY=\\\"value\\\"`, skip blanks. Do not pull in python-dotenv.\\n\\nHappy-path byte-identity is a MUST: when all keys alive, the existing distribution at ~evals/parallel.py:558-579 must produce identical worker\u2192key assignments as before. Keep filtering order stable.\\n\\nCLI budget: default timeout=5.0s, concurrency=8, so N\u22648 keys finish in one batch \u22645s, well under the 10s target.\\n\\nDo not touch megaplan/key_pool.py, iteration-021/022 data, or _preflight_check_megaplan() placement. Keep the diff tight.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository’s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
